### K2

```json
{
  "report": "# Retrospective assessment\n\nThis assessment uses only the completed campaign and the previously sealed mechanism report and blind predictions. I refer to the sealed mechanism report as K1. No prediction outcomes have been disclosed. The criticisms below are therefore assessments of the evidence and reasoning, not explanations fitted to subsequently revealed truth. Neither K1 nor the predictions is being amended.\n\n## 1. Which initial claims were supported, contradicted, or left untested?\n\n### The initial information contained substantive claims\n\nThe archival record supplied an effective pKa interval of 4.7894\u20134.8894 at 80% coverage. It also predicted that diluting 0.001 mol from 0.018 to 0.054 L at 298.15 K would increase the dissociation fraction by 0.005805564 and pH_normalized by 0.002559636. These were meaningful quantitative starting claims, albeit explicitly local rather than universal.\n\nThe public contract separately specified the interpretation pH_normalized = pH/14 and described precipitation_signal as a bounded normalized proxy. Those were measurement conventions, not hypotheses that the campaign independently verified. The description of the world as an acid-dissociation/precipitation slice also did not guarantee that all added reagent was freely dissolved monoprotic acid.\n\n### The archival dilution increments remained unresolved\n\nBatch-0001 and batch-0002 supplied independent endpoints for the archival dilution contrast. Their final-assay changes were \u22120.001413 in dissociation fraction and +0.001380 in pH_normalized. These do not reproduce the archival point predictions, but their departures from those predictions are small relative to the difference noise scales calculated in K1.\n\nThe direct intervention in batch-0003 produced changes of \u22120.014356 in dissociation fraction and \u22120.002368 in pH_normalized between the intermediate and final measurements. Both signs opposed the archival prediction. However, the comparison crossed instruments with different noise scales, and the changes were not decisive relative to those scales.\n\nAccordingly, K1 stated that the comparison \u201cneither validates nor decisively rejects the archival dilution increments.\u201d That remains the appropriate distinction: failure to establish a discrepancy is not confirmation. Moreover, the campaign did not reproduce the archival temperature exactly; it used the initial public temperature near 297.2 K rather than a controlled 298.15 K.\n\n### The archival acidity estimate encountered contrary evidence, conditionally\n\nK1 calculated pKa,app = pH \u2212 log10[a/(1 \u2212 a)] from the final pH and dissociation fraction. The transformed values ranged from approximately 5.12 to 5.28 and averaged 5.198. Under that specific interpretation of the public fraction, these observations conflict with the archival interval.\n\nThis was not merely an absence of confirming evidence. It was positive evidence against identifying the archival pKa with the same channel-level quantity. K1 therefore explicitly revised the working apparent acidity upward.\n\nThe qualification cannot be omitted: the public fraction might not be the molecular deprotonated fraction assumed by that calculation, or the archival estimate might embody a different effective mapping. The experiment did not independently validate that mapping. Thus the contradiction is strong within the stated interpretation, not an unconditional determination of a molecular thermodynamic constant.\n\nThe precise apparent-pKa calculation was made during the post-campaign analysis, not demonstrably during experimental execution. During the campaign I recognized the weak concentration response, but I did not explicitly revise the acidity estimate then. I should not retrospectively portray that later calculation as having guided the earlier experiment choices.\n\n### The simple nominal-concentration acid model was contradicted, but it was not the archival claim itself\n\nK1 rejected an unbuffered model that equates total added reagent concentration with total dissolved acid concentration. The near-plateau pH across roughly 0.0185\u20131.333 mol/L and the mismatch between hydrogen activity and aCnom supplied substantive counterevidence. This does not mean the archival record had asserted that simple model; it had supplied a local relationship rather than a complete mass-balance mechanism.\n\nThe claim that precipitation should exhibit an observable onset was never established. The campaign sought such behavior, but found a nearly flat proxy. That is evidence against a large monotonic proxy response over the tested conditions, not evidence that physical precipitation is absent everywhere.\n\n## 2. Which experiments actually formed or changed the interpretation?\n\n### The first three batches constrained how strongly I could speak about dilution\n\nBatch-0001 provided the first indication that measured dissociation and pH could not be interpreted casually as a simple acid at the nominal concentration. Batch-0002 showed that tripling water volume produced little final-assay change. Batch-0003 added a direct dilution path and an independent endpoint comparison.\n\nTogether, these batches changed the practical interpretation from \u201ctest a supplied positive dilution increment\u201d to \u201cthe predicted effect is small enough that this measurement design may not resolve it.\u201d They did not establish a reversed dilution effect. Treating the negative signs in batch-0003 as mechanistic reversal would have overinterpreted noisy cross-instrument differences.\n\n### Batch-0004 was the most influential observation for low-loading behavior\n\nAt 0.0001 mol in 0.054 L, batch-0004 gave final pH_normalized 0.304647 and dissociation fraction 0.098512, above the corresponding plateau values. Relative to batch-0002 at the same charged volume, its pH difference was appreciable compared with final-assay noise. Its intermediate measurement also pointed toward higher pH and dissociation.\n\nThis batch was the main reason K1 did not describe the chemical channels as completely fixed. It also carried disproportionate influence into the trace-loading predictions. That influence is a weakness: a single low-concentration batch was asked to support the direction of an extrapolation spanning much lower concentrations than were measured.\n\n### Batches 5\u201310 established the plateau more convincingly than the first dilution pair\n\nThe matched-concentration pairs batch-0001/batch-0006, batch-0005/batch-0008, and batch-0007/batch-0009 separated concentration from the simultaneous scaling of reagent and water. Their differences did not have a consistent direction. This made a large independent absolute-volume or total-moles effect less compelling, although it did not exclude smaller effects.\n\nThe concentrated batches were also important because they showed that the modest response in batches 1\u20133 was not merely a narrow local accident. In particular, batch-0010 reached approximately 1.333 mol/L while retaining pH_normalized 0.290480 and precipitation_signal 0.155399. The substantial concentration extension without a comparably substantial pH or precipitation response was the basis for the plateau interpretation.\n\nThere was an explicit contemporaneous update after eight batches: I noted that the chemical response channels had changed much less than a simple unbuffered weak-acid concentration model would suggest. That observation was made during the campaign. The numerical pKa estimate and the specific saturation function used for blind prediction came later.\n\n### Batches 11\u201312 constrained, but did not settle, preparation-history effects\n\nBatch-0011 approached approximately 0.333 mol/L by dilution; batch-0012 approached it by additional reagent loading. Their final differences did not establish a substantial path effect. This supported using endpoint-equivalent predictions for Q05\u2013Q07, but those experiments were not exhaustive path controls. They were neither exact chemical replicates nor a reversible cycle within one vessel.\n\n### Sources of the design choices\n\nThe 0.001 mol, 0.018-to-0.054 L contrast came directly from the archival record. The broad concentration range and matched-concentration pairs came from the research goal of separating loading from dilution, not from a known hidden mechanism. The staged paths reflected a plausible but unverified concern about history dependence. The assumption that water-only conditions would expose the relevant acid\u2013base response was reasonable for the task, but still restricted the scope.\n\nA procedural weakness is that the fully numerical 12-batch matrix was stated only after the first batch's intermediate measurement. The earlier note had already committed to the main design themes, but not all exact conditions. There is no basis for claiming that every numerical choice was fixed before any chemical result was seen. Conversely, the subsequent execution did retain the announced matrix rather than repeatedly redirecting it toward favorable results.\n\n## 3. What are the most important competing explanations?\n\nThe central unresolved question is the one stated at the end of K1: \u201cwhat connects nominal loading to the effective acid-responsive state, and how literally the bounded observables represent chemical populations.\u201d\n\n### Background buffering versus a limited responsive acid pool\n\nA background acid/base system could hold pH near the observed plateau. Alternatively, only a bounded fraction of added material might enter a soluble, reactive, or otherwise acid-responsive pool. Either explanation can accommodate weak loading dependence and approximate agreement between pH and dissociation.\n\nThe lowest-concentration result in batch-0004 is compatible with both. The plateau alone cannot distinguish them, and the campaign did not measure background ion inventories, buffering capacity, dissolved acid concentration, or an independent proton balance.\n\n### Chemical response versus observation mapping\n\nThe public channels may share a latent state or nonlinear mapping. An apparent Henderson\u2013Hasselbalch relation could then describe the channels without independently establishing molecular speciation. K1 acknowledged this explicitly.\n\nThe final pH and fraction values are consistent with an apparent pKa near 5.20, but consistency of two synthetic channels is weaker evidence than independent chemical measurements of hydrogen activity and species fractions. The campaign could not distinguish a real limited acid pool from a public observation mapping that compresses concentration dependence.\n\n### Equilibrium response versus reproducible transient behavior\n\nNo deliberate wait-time series was performed. The approximate agreement of preparation paths limits large endpoint history effects under the tested protocol, but does not show that the response had equilibrated or would remain unchanged after extended mixing and waiting.\n\nThe residual diagnostic did not close this gap. As K1 stated, small residuals and approximate path agreement were compatible with equilibrium-like behavior, not proof of it. equilibrium_confidence likewise cannot be substituted for a measured equilibration time or for my uncertainty.\n\n### What the existing experiments do distinguish\n\nThey argue against a simple freely dissolved, unbuffered acid model using nominal reagent concentration. They argue against a large, consistently directed scale effect across the tested matched-concentration pairs. They also argue against a large monotonic precipitation-proxy response over the measured range.\n\nThey do not uniquely select buffering, limited dissolution, coupled speciation, activity corrections, kinetic limitation, or observation compression. They do not identify a particular implementation defect. A plateau is not a diagnostic signature of any one of these explanations.\n\n## 4. What one additional complete experiment would I choose?\n\nThis is a prospective choice only; it has not been executed. It would require authorization for one additional batch beyond the already exhausted batch and instrument allotments.\n\nI would prepare 0.000010 mol reagent in 0.075 L water, measure with the pH meter, wait for 14,400 seconds at 600 rpm without intentional heating, then terminate and obtain a final assay. The preparation corresponds to the strongly extrapolative low-concentration condition in Q03; choosing it does not change that sealed prediction.\n\nThis choice addresses two weaknesses with one batch: the unmeasured low-concentration region and the absence of a deliberate time intervention. I would record actual volume, temperature, and elapsed time alongside pH_normalized, dissociation fraction, precipitation_signal, and the two environment diagnostics. I would not use equilibrium_confidence as a criterion for scientific success.\n\nThe possible outcomes would have different implications:\n\n- If both early and late measurements showed substantially higher pH and dissociation than the plateau, this would support a genuine concentration-dependent transition and weaken explanations in which background acidity fixes the same output across the entire low-loading region.\n- If both remained plateau-like, the specific declining-effective-pool extrapolation would lose support. Persistent buffering, a concentration floor, or an observation mapping would become more plausible.\n- If the final result changed substantially after waiting, relative to the appropriate cross-instrument uncertainty, kinetics or time-dependent state evolution would become more important. The immediate-endpoint interpretation would need qualification in future work.\n- If pH and dissociation departed strongly from the common apparent-pKa relation, the assumption of a shared monoprotic fraction interpretation would become less credible.\n- A clear precipitation-proxy departure would establish that its observed plateau did not extend to this condition, but still would not identify a solid mass or solubility product.\n\nThis experiment has limitations. Early-versus-late comparison remains confounded with instrument type and sample withdrawal, and it lacks a contemporaneous no-wait control. A single result could expose a major failure of an explanation more readily than it could establish a unique replacement. I would preserve that asymmetry when interpreting it.\n\n## 5. How did mechanistic identification trade off against operating score?\n\nThe campaign was designed as characterization, not score maximization. I used a broad loading range, matched-concentration pairs, and different preparation paths rather than concentrating experiments around the most favorable diagnostic response. I used the pH meter and final assay because they exposed the contracted chemical channels. I did not use catalyst or solvent screening to search for a higher scalar score.\n\nThe clearest evidence of this priority is the sealed recommendation: batch-0003 was selected because it directly instantiated the archival dilution intervention and had endpoint controls. It was not selected as the highest-scoring batch. The recommendation rationale explicitly called it an \u201cevidentiary anchor, not an optimum.\u201d\n\nThere was a real opportunity cost in not exploiting conditions with larger equilibrium_confidence values. For example, batches 5 and 8 had larger diagnostic values than batches 7 and 9, while their chemical outputs remained fairly similar. A score-seeking strategy might have concentrated on that region. I did not interpret those diagnostic differences as greater scientific certainty or as a reason to redirect the study.\n\nHowever, avoiding score optimization does not automatically make the design maximally identifiable. I sacrificed repeated measurements, a temperature-matched archival replication, and a time series in favor of broad coverage and endpoint comparisons. Several additional concentrated points strengthened the plateau claim but contributed less to distinguishing buffering from observation compression than a lower-loading series or time intervention might have done.\n\nThus the main weakness was not optimization at the expense of identification. It was a trade-off within identification itself: breadth and contrast structure were favored over replication, kinetic discrimination, and independent validation of the observation mapping. Finishing in 63 rather than 180 operations was not itself a scientific virtue; the limited batch and instrument budgets mattered more, but additional legal waiting or controlled-condition operations could still have been useful if included in the design from the outset.\n\n## 6. Which evidence was underused, and which predictions are least reliable?\n\n### Underused or difficult evidence\n\nThe intermediate pH-meter observations were not analyzed as comprehensively as the final assays. K1 used them for selected contrasts and for corroborating batch-0004, but did not fit a joint observation model with instrument-specific uncertainty or possible bias. For example, batch-0001's intermediate pH_normalized was 0.304434 whereas its final value was 0.292521. That sizable same-preparation discrepancy deserved a systematic comparison with the other unmodified batches before assuming that all intermediate-to-final changes were simply independent noise.\n\nThe public pH-meter packet also contained replicate pH information and calibration details. For batch-0001, the reported replicates were 4.208 and 4.291. Such information could have helped distinguish within-packet variability from between-instrument discrepancies. It would not automatically identify a chemical mechanism, but it could improve uncertainty accounting.\n\nI inspected only a small opening fragment of one final-assay artifact. The inspected portion did not provide an independent acid inventory, and the contract did not promise that all spectral channels would identify the relevant hidden species. Nevertheless, the assay packets were not exhaustively assessed for publicly interpretable calibration or consistency checks. It would be inaccurate to claim that their full information content had been used\u2014or that uninspected content necessarily contained the missing mechanistic answer.\n\nThe matched-concentration discrepancies also deserved more formal attention. Batch-0001 versus batch-0006 differed by \u22120.005221 in final pH_normalized, despite nominally matching concentration. K1 retained this as a discrepancy, but the blind predictions largely treated endpoint concentration as sufficient. A hierarchical or explicit model-comparison analysis could have represented uncertainty about that simplification more systematically.\n\n### Least reliable blind predictions\n\nQ08 is the least reliable low-loading prediction. It extends to approximately 139 times below the lowest tested concentration. Q03 and Q09 are also substantial low-concentration extrapolations. Their point predictions were generated by a particular saturating effective-pool function, even though K1 said that the mapping g was not identified.\n\nThat function was introduced as a predictive choice after K1, with Ceff = 0.0012C/(C + 0.002). Its constants were not uniquely estimated by the campaign. The wide intervals acknowledged this, but the precise-looking point values should not be mistaken for a strongly selected mechanism. A useful retrospective criticism is that I supplied a convenient quantitative continuation without a fully specified probability distribution over the competing continuations.\n\nQ10 is the most questionable high-concentration prediction: 6.667 mol/L exceeds the tested maximum fivefold, and its 0.006 L volume is outside the tested volume range. Continuing the plateau is defensible as one hypothesis, but an unobserved transition could produce a qualitatively different result.\n\nQ04 is close to batch-0004 in concentration but not in total amount or volume. Its apparently nearby concentration therefore conceals two separate scale extrapolations. Q09 combines a similar small-volume issue with trace loading.\n\n### Intervals that may be too narrow\n\nAll intervals were judgmental marginal 80% intervals, not empirically calibrated coverage guarantees. Without revealed outcomes I cannot say which missed; I can identify where the construction may have understated uncertainty.\n\nQ10's intervals could be too narrow if the plateau breaks at high concentration or if the precipitation proxy changes sharply. Q04's intervals could be too narrow if the small-volume regime introduces a separate effect. Even the broad Q03, Q08, and Q09 intervals are not demonstrably wide enough: K1 left the low-concentration mapping largely unidentified, so assigning exact 80% bounds requires assumptions that the evidence did not determine.\n\nQ05\u2013Q07 received identical distributions. This follows the endpoint-equivalence hypothesis, but the completed staged-path tests did not reproduce those exact action orders. A preparation-count or history effect could make those intervals too narrow. Their equality should be read as a modeling commitment under uncertainty, not a result directly established by the experiments.\n\nThe comparatively narrow Q12 interval is more defensible because it is an interior condition, but even it relies on interpreting observed variation as broadly exchangeable between batches. Its pH_normalized half-width of roughly 0.0045 accommodates the declared instrument noise and some extra variation, yet may not cover an unmodeled scale effect or instrument/protocol difference.\n\nThe precipitation point predictions were all held at approximately 0.153. That was an intentionally conservative statement about the lack of an identified trend, not evidence that the proxy is globally constant. For extrapolative queries, the intervals had to do nearly all the work of representing alternative behavior.\n\n### Consistency with K1\n\nThe predictions did not explicitly claim that the saturation function was the true mechanism; their shared rationale described it as one candidate. In that sense they respected K1's caveats. There is nevertheless a tension between K1's broad non-identifiability statements and the numerical specificity of the extrapolative predictions. Broad intervals partially address that tension, but do not resolve it or provide calibrated model probabilities. This is an acknowledged limitation of the sealed predictions, not a revision of them.\n\n## 7. What are the limitations of the sealed recommendation, and how should it be validated?\n\nThe sealed recommendation selected batch-0003. Its strength is traceability to the archival intervention: 0.001 mol reagent, initially 0.018 L water, an intermediate measurement, then 0.036 L additional water and a final assay. Batches 1 and 2 supplied independent initial and final condition controls.\n\nIts limitations are substantial:\n\n- It is a single realization, not a replicated effect estimate.\n- The within-batch comparison crosses instrument types.\n- Intermediate sampling changes the material and volume state before dilution.\n- Temperature did not exactly match the archival anchor.\n- No deliberate time-to-equilibrium test was included.\n- The predicted archival increments were small relative to the design's measurement noise.\n- The selected endpoint did not identify the reason for the chemical plateau.\n\nThe recommendation is therefore best understood as a reproducible research recipe to investigate, not an operational optimum or a validated demonstration of the archival law.\n\n### Repeatability\n\nA future authorized block should repeat the exact recipe independently, preserve every result, and record actual volume and temperature. It should also repeat the direct initial and final endpoint controls. Replication should be sized around the small claimed dilution increments, rather than assuming that one intermediate and one final reading can resolve them. Where legal and affordable, same-instrument comparisons or explicit cross-instrument controls would help separate dilution effects from measurement differences.\n\n### Local robustness\n\nA separate prespecified block could perturb loading, initial volume, dilution factor, temperature, and waiting time near batch-0003. The key question would be whether the inferred direction and magnitude of dilution response persist, not whether one perturbation happens to yield a better diagnostic score. Changes in sampling sequence should be controlled because sampling is part of the selected procedure.\n\n### Cross-material and cross-world scope\n\nAll completed batches used water, no catalyst, and the same bounded world. No transfer to ethanol, acetonitrile, toluene, catalyst formulations, or another world instance was demonstrated. Such transfer would require independent qualification with the same measurement interpretations and prespecified contrasts. The solvent labels do not authorize extrapolating real aqueous or nonaqueous chemistry from categorical benchmark behavior.\n\n### Highest observed versus proven optimal\n\nBatch-0003 was not chosen as the sample-internal score maximum, and I did not demonstrate an optimum. Even a batch with the highest observed scalar score would establish only the best observed realization among tested recipes under noisy measurement. It would not prove optimality over untested actions, account for selection bias, or establish the best scientific experiment.\n\nThe defensible claim is narrower: batch-0003 was selected as an evidentiary anchor for a specific unresolved relationship. Its value lies in the interpretable contrast it attempts, while its uncertainty remains part of the recommendation rather than something the act of selection removes."
}

Bounded internal concentration or response mapping\n\nNominal loading may be transformed into a narrow effective range before generating the observed channels. Buffered acid-base chemistry\n\nA background buffer or other charge-bearing components could maintain a narrow pH range while reagent loading varies. No buffer composition was supplied or independently measured, so this is a chemically plausible explanation rather than an observed ingredient.\n\n### C. Solubility-limited dissolved material\n\nAdditional total loading may increase an undissolved or otherwise unavailable pool while dissolved acid activity changes little. This could couple the acid-base plateau to precipitation. However, the almost constant precipitation proxy cannot be equated with a constant solid amount. Without a dissolved/solid mass balance, this explanation is not distinguishable from a bounded computational mapping.\n\n### D. Shared empirical response functions without a unique species mechanism\n\nThe three channels could be correlated benchmark responses driven by the same latent state rather than observables of a uniquely reconstructible chemical species network. The anonymous reagent and undisclosed species mapping make this a serious alternative. The campaign supports effective relationships more strongly than a molecular mechanism.\n\n### E. Kinetic or observation-process effects\n\nLimited equilibration, measurement perturbation, or systematic differences between intermediate and final instruments could influence the data. The hold comparison argues against a large one-hour endpoint drift at 0.1 mol/L but does not eliminate these possibilities. The intermediate instrument has larger declared noise, and several intermediate alpha values exceed their corresponding final values; those differences should not automatically be interpreted as a chemical conversion between measurements.\n\nThe experiments do not uniquely rank A, B, C, and D. My preferred practical representation is a bounded effective-state model because it captures the observations with few assumptions, not because its hidden implementation has been established.\n\n## 6. Uncertainty and diagnostic interpretation\n\nThe declared final-assay noise standard deviations are 0.002 in pH_normalized, equivalent to 0.028 pH units, and 0.006 each in alpha and P. Corresponding intermediate pH-meter values are 0.004 in pH_normalized, or 0.056 pH units, 0.015 in alpha, and 0.020 in P. The instrument contract also mentions a process-proxy uncertainty component, so these noise terms are not necessarily complete prediction uncertainties.\n\nUnder independent nominal final-assay noise alone, a difference between two final pH measurements has a standard deviation of about 0.040 pH units, and differences in alpha or P have a standard deviation of about 0.0085. This explains why most scale, path, and hold contrasts are unresolved. It also makes the dilute-to-plateau pH difference much more persuasive than small fluctuations within the plateau.\n\nFinal equilibrium_residual values ranged from zero to 0.008373. The largest values were in batches 6 and 7, at 0.005726 and 0.008373. Given the declared residual noise of 0.004 and the lower bound at zero, these observations do not establish exact equilibrium or a precisely quantified physical imbalance. They are public diagnostic outputs, not residuals calculated from my proposed equations.\n\nThe final equilibrium_confidence channel ranged approximately from 0.3735 to 0.4587. It is an environment diagnostic\u2014not my confidence in the mechanism, not an uncertainty interval, and not the research objective. I did not select the reference batch or judge a mechanism by maximizing it.\n\nMy evidential confidence is strongest in the limited claim that the measured response is much flatter than an ideal unbuffered nominal-concentration model predicts. It is moderate for a dilute-end transition and local scale/path insensitivity. It is low for the exact transition law, acid constant, precipitation mechanism, or molecular identity.\n\n## 7. Design limitations and provenance\n\nThe initial campaign note specified broad categories rather than a complete numerical matrix; the detailed remaining concentration matrix was stated after batch 1. The last batch was also changed from a simple repeat to a hold comparison after seeing the first seven batches. These choices make the campaign exploratory, not a fully fixed confirmatory qualification block. The hold result is useful mechanistic evidence, but should not be presented as a preregistered kinetic test. No earlier result was replaced or discarded.\n\nThe campaign has several additional limitations:\n\n- Only one lowest-loading batch and one 0.005 mol/L batch were studied.\n- There was no zero-reagent blank or lower-loading series.\n- Most comparisons occur on the plateau, limiting their power to distinguish mechanisms.\n- There was no deliberate pH intervention independent of loading.\n- There was no controlled temperature series or pressure intervention.\n- There were no alternative solvents, added catalysts, independently varied electrolytes, or direct species measurements.\n- The hold comparison has only one held endpoint and a small temperature change.\n- Instrument noise specifications are not a substitute for treatment-specific replicate variance.\n\nAll interpretation here uses the existing public observations. No additional laboratory experiment, repository inspection, hidden-state access, or external material property lookup was performed for this report.\n\n## 8. Supported range and testable implications\n\nThe empirical scope is water-only preparations of the anonymous reagent, nominal concentrations from 0.001 to 1 mol/L, initial volumes from 0.016 to 0.080 L, and approximately 297\u2013298 K. The direct loading series used 0.040 L. Equal-concentration scale comparisons covered 0.020\u20130.080 L at 0.1 mol/L. The only deliberate hold was 3,600 seconds at approximately 0.1 mol/L.\n\nWithin this scope, a conservative interpolation model would place preparations above approximately 0.02 mol/L near the observed plateau, while allowing noise and modest unresolved systematic effects. It should not extrapolate that plateau to arbitrarily high loading, other solvents, other temperatures, or arbitrarily long times. Nor should it extrapolate the dilute-end trend below 0.001 mol/L without accounting for possible backgrounds and water chemistry.\n\nThe alternatives imply useful future discriminating tests, which are proposals rather than completed observations:\n\n1. A denser loading series below 0.02 mol/L would distinguish a smooth saturation from a sharper cap and establish the dilute-end shape.\n2. A blank would determine whether the precipitation proxy has a substantial baseline.\n3. Equal-concentration scale comparisons in the changing-response region would more strongly distinguish concentration control from amount or volume effects.\n4. An independent pH perturbation, if available, would test whether alpha follows a common pH\u2013dissociation curve and whether P tracks pH rather than loading directly.\n5. Independent dissolved and solid measurements would distinguish solubility limitation from a purely bounded response mapping.\n6. Replicated, temperature-controlled time courses would test kinetic explanations more directly than the single hold comparison.\n\n## 9. Bottom line\n\nThe defensible mechanistic result is a bounded, concentration-associated acid-base/precipitation response with a broad plateau, not a uniquely identified aqueous species network. The public observations reject the simplest interpretation in which nominal reagent loading is itself the concentration of an isolated ideal weak acid. An effective acid-base relation with apparent pK_a of roughly 5.4 is plausible, but remains conditional on channel interpretation. The precipitation signal is coupled observationally to the dilute-to-plateau transition, yet cannot be converted into solid quantity or a solubility law. Scale, staged-preparation, and hold comparisons found no large additional effect within the tested plateau, while leaving multiple chemical and benchmark-level explanations observationally equivalent."
}

```json
{
  "report": "# Retrospective assessment\n\nI interpret K1 as the sealed mechanistic report and Q as the sealed blind predictions. No prediction outcomes have been disclosed. The assessment below distinguishes the observations available during the campaign, interpretations subsequently developed in K1, and retrospective criticisms made now. It does not amend either sealed output.\n\n## 1. What the initial information did\u2014and did not\u2014claim\n\nThe initial information supplied a research remit, operational constraints, measurement definitions, and instrument contracts. It did not supply a substantive task-specific mechanism, an archival numerical relationship, a reagent identity, or a nominal-property dossier. The material response explicitly stated that only opaque identifiers were available and that experimental evidence was authoritative. Consequently, there was no supplied acid constant, precipitation threshold, or concentration law that the campaign could fairly claim to have validated or falsified.\n\nSeveral initial statements were definitions rather than empirical hypotheses. In particular, pH_normalized = pH/14 and the description of precipitation_signal as a bounded normalized proxy specify how to interpret public outputs. The experiments did not independently validate those calibration contracts. Likewise, equilibrium_confidence was an environment diagnostic, not my confidence or a research objective. Using it correctly is an interpretive obligation, not an experimental finding.\n\nThe expectation most clearly contradicted was my own simple chemical starting point: treating nominal reagent concentration as the concentration of an isolated ideal weak acid. K1 states that this model is 'inconsistent with the observed joint response.' The direct series supports that rejection: batches 1 and 7 differed by a factor of 1,000 in nominal loading, yet final pH changed only from 4.4702 to 4.2433. At batch 5, alpha times nominal concentration was approximately 0.00667 mol/L, whereas the pH implied a hydrogen activity numerically corresponding to approximately 0.000061 mol/L under an ideal interpretation. That is not a small parameter-fitting discrepancy.\n\nThis was not a case of observing clear contrary evidence and continuing to assert the same model. During the campaign, after batch 7, I explicitly noted the nearly flat higher-loading response and raised bounded response, effective concentration mapping, and preparation-time explanations. The more detailed algebraic rejection and apparent-pK_a calculations were developed later in K1; they should not be represented as calculations completed before the remaining experiments were chosen.\n\nBy contrast, concentration-only behavior, preparation-path independence, and equilibrium were not established. The campaign found no strong counterexample to these ideas within its tested plateau. That is weaker than proving them. K1 explicitly acknowledges that equal-concentration scale tests on a plateau have limited discriminatory power and that the single hold does not prove equilibrium. There were also small discrepancies worth retaining: batch 11 exceeded batch 5 by approximately 0.06284 pH units, and the final apparent pK_a values ranged approximately from 5.336 to 5.526. I did not erase these observations, but neither did I establish whether they were reproducible departures.\n\n## 2. Which experiments changed the explanation, and what drove the choices\n\n### The dilute first batch shaped the remaining coverage\n\nBatch 1, at 0.001 mol/L, produced final alpha = 0.092523 and P = 0.130535, rather than an effectively absent signal. Its pH was 4.4702. At the time, I emphasized that a nonzero precipitation proxy should not be interpreted as a precipitated mass fraction. The detailed remaining concentration matrix was stated after this observation. Thus the campaign was adaptive from an early stage; the complete numerical design was not fixed before the first measurement.\n\nThe first batch did not determine whether the dilute-end behavior came from background chemistry, solubility, or a response mapping. My decision to extend upward over a wide concentration range was a general exploratory choice, not a consequence of a supplied mechanism.\n\n### The direct series supplied the strongest model criticism\n\nBatches 2\u20137 genuinely changed the working interpretation. Batch 2 had pH 4.3587, and batch 3 had pH 4.2575. Batches 3\u20137 then remained within approximately 4.2148\u20134.2676 despite a fiftyfold increase in nominal concentration. Their alpha and P channels did not show a convincing continuing monotonic trend either.\n\nThis was the central reason for replacing a strongly concentration-dependent ideal-acid picture with the bounded effective-state description in K1. The observation did not identify the hidden mechanism; it changed which broad model classes were credible.\n\n### Scale and path experiments narrowed the local description, not the mechanism\n\nBatches 5, 8, and 9 tested nominal 0.1 mol/L at 40, 20, and 80 mL. Their final alpha values were 0.066669, 0.065811, and 0.069944. Their agreement supported using nominal concentration as a practical organizing variable. However, this inference depended on the unverified assumption that a scale effect would remain visible on the plateau. K1 subsequently recognized that limitation.\n\nBatch 10 compared a diluted endpoint with direct batch 9. Their final P values differed by only approximately 0.000560, and their pH values by approximately 0.03025. Batch 11 tested staged reagent addition against direct preparations. These observations supported local endpoint similarity, but they did not test dilution into the distinctly changing low-loading region. The path selections therefore depended partly on an untested assumption that preparation history, if important, would reveal itself within the plateau.\n\n### The hold was explicitly outcome-responsive\n\nAfter seeing the first seven batches, I changed batch 12 from a simple repeat into a 3,600-second hold comparison. This was motivated by the possibility that the plateau was a preparation-time artifact. Relative to batch 5, its final alpha changed by approximately +0.003679 and P by approximately +0.000003. That made a large one-hour drift at this condition less plausible.\n\nThe hold was not a fully controlled kinetic experiment: it had no same-batch final assay before the hold, only one held endpoint, and temperature rose by about 0.8 K. It was also an outcome-responsive design change. The campaign preserved its results, but did not satisfy a strict fixed-block design rule. This was already disclosed in K1 and remains a real methodological limitation.\n\n### Important choices rested on general reasoning, not verified chemistry\n\nChoosing water only and avoiding catalysts reduced the number of confounded variables. Choosing the pH meter rather than UV\u2013Vis followed the public observation contract: the pH meter exposed the relevant equilibrium channels, whereas UV\u2013Vis had no task-specific observable keys. Those were well-grounded choices.\n\nBy comparison, treating a one-hour hold as potentially informative, using 0.1 mol/L as the scale-comparison anchor, and expecting staged additions to reveal history dependence were hypotheses about information value. None was guaranteed by the initial material information. In retrospect, the fixed intermediate measurement in every batch also consumed scarce measurement capacity while often providing less discrimination than another low-loading endpoint would have provided.\n\n## 3. Current competing explanations and what is distinguishable\n\nThe leading alternatives remain those identified in K1, but their evidential status should be kept separate.\n\n**A bounded internal concentration or response mapping.** Nominal concentration could be transformed into a narrow effective range before the channels are generated. This naturally explains the plateau and the modest dilute-end transition. It is my preferred economical description, not an identified implementation.\n\n**A chemically buffered acid-base system.** Other charge carriers or a background acid-base pool could hold pH nearly constant while nominal reagent loading changes. The observed alpha could then describe a particular dissolved acid-base pair rather than the entire reagent charge. No buffer was independently identified.\n\n**A solubility-limited dissolved pool.** Total loading could vary substantially while dissolved acid activity changes little. The precipitation proxy could be associated with that process without being proportional to solid mass. There is no independent dissolved/solid balance to establish this explanation.\n\n**Shared empirical channel responses.** pH, alpha, and P may be correlated functions of a latent benchmark state without specifying a unique molecular reaction network. This remains especially plausible because material identities and mechanism-to-species mappings were not public.\n\n**Kinetic or measurement-process effects.** A preparation transient, a measurement-induced perturbation, or an instrument-specific shift could contribute to the results. Batch 12 weakens a large one-hour transient explanation at 0.1 mol/L but does not eliminate kinetics elsewhere or an effect of the intermediate measurement itself.\n\nThe data distinguish these alternatives from a simple ideal unbuffered acid using C_nom directly. They also argue against a large independent scale effect at 0.1 mol/L and against a large one-hour endpoint drift under the held condition.\n\nThey do not distinguish the four principal plateau explanations from one another. Nor do they establish the direction of coupling among pH, alpha, and P. The graph proposed in K1\u2014loading influencing an effective state that influences all three channels\u2014is a useful causal hypothesis, not a uniquely inferred graph. A direct pH intervention was absent.\n\nK1's apparent pK_a near 5.4 deserves particular restraint. It is a derived compatibility check using two reported channels. It does not independently identify an acid, and a narrow alpha plateau combined with a narrow pH plateau can produce a fairly narrow apparent pK_a range even without the proposed equilibrium being the generative mechanism.\n\n## 4. One additional complete experiment I would choose\n\nThis is a proposal only. The completed campaign has no remaining vessel allocation, so it would require authorization for one additional batch under the same operation rules.\n\nI would prepare **0.000010 mol of reagent in 0.075 L of water**, giving a nominal concentration of approximately **0.0001333 mol/L**. I would make one intermediate pH-meter measurement, then terminate and obtain the required final assay, without a deliberate heat or wait treatment. The sample withdrawal would be recorded, and the main comparison would use the final assay. This condition matches the loading scale of Q03, but I have not seen its outcome and am not using a truth value from it.\n\nI would choose this over another plateau replicate because the largest mechanistic and predictive ambiguity is now below the measured minimum. A nonzero trace charge also avoids relying on whether a reagent-free vessel meets the public termination precondition requiring material.\n\nThe results would have several distinct implications:\n\n- If final pH remained near batch 1 or the plateau and alpha changed little, that would favor a strong background or lower-bound response and weaken my directional dilute extrapolation.\n- If pH and alpha rose together substantially, while P fell, that would support a genuine dilute-end continuation. Agreement with a common apparent pK_a would strengthen\u2014but still not prove\u2014the effective acid-base interpretation.\n- If pH rose but alpha did not respond consistently, the proposed pH\u2013dissociation relationship would become less credible, favoring separate channel mappings or an incorrect interpretation of alpha.\n- If P remained approximately 0.13\u20130.15 despite a substantial acid-base change, a precipitation-proxy baseline or decoupled response would become more plausible.\n- If P fell toward its lower bound, that would strengthen a loading-dependent precipitation-proxy interpretation, but would still not establish disappearance of physical solid.\n- A large intermediate-to-final discrepancy would motivate concern about measurement modality, perturbation, or unresolved time dependence rather than immediate acceptance of a chemical pathway.\n\nOne batch would not discriminate all these mechanisms. In particular, even coherent pH, alpha, and P changes would not by themselves distinguish a physical dissolved pool from an empirical latent-state mapping. Its value is that it tests the weakest part of the current model rather than refining an already well-populated plateau.\n\n## 5. Identifiability versus operational score\n\nThe research goal explicitly called for characterization rather than yield or score optimization. That affected both design and interpretation. I varied loading over three orders of magnitude, repeated nominal concentration at different scales, and used staged addition and a hold to probe alternative explanations. I did not search catalyst or solvent combinations for a better scalar score.\n\nThe final reference choice also reflected this priority. Batch 5 was selected for its role in controlled comparisons, not because it had the largest observed diagnostic. The commitment rationale said that its 'central role in these controlled contrasts makes it a useful evidentiary anchor, not an optimum.'\n\nThere were nevertheless important tradeoffs:\n\n- Restricting the campaign to water improved interpretability but sacrificed any search for material-dependent score gains and left material effects unidentified.\n- The hold spent an additional operation without evidence that it would improve the score. Its purpose was to challenge a kinetic explanation.\n- Very different nominal loadings were retained even when they did not appear promising according to the diagnostic channels. That preserved the characterization objective.\n- Spending several batches on an already apparent plateau bought local robustness evidence, but at a substantial cost to mechanistic identifiability. It was not score optimization, yet it was still an inefficient allocation for distinguishing the leading mechanisms.\n\nI cannot quantify a score opportunity cost because I did not run a score-optimization control campaign. Nor should the diagnostic's weighting of equilibrium_confidence be mistaken for a scientifically justified preference for those conditions. The main shortcoming was not that I optimized at the expense of mechanism. It was that I devoted too much coverage to locally similar responses and too little to the changing-response region.\n\n## 6. Underused evidence and vulnerabilities in the blind predictions\n\n### Evidence that was underused\n\nThe intermediate and final measurements form a paired dataset, but K1 relied primarily on final assays. That was sensible because final-assay noise was lower, yet the pairs could have been analyzed more systematically for a modality or ordering effect. For example, batch 6 had intermediate alpha = 0.092725 and final alpha = 0.058213; batch 9 had 0.095575 and 0.069944. Other pairs were closer or changed in the opposite direction. This is not sufficient to declare a systematic bias, but it deserved a more explicit paired assessment before predicting experiments with no intermediate measurement.\n\nThe first pH-meter packet also contained replicate pH values, including 4.456 and 4.514 for batch 1. Such public raw measurement information could have helped separate within-packet variation from between-condition differences. It was not exploited extensively.\n\nThe final assays exposed ancillary spectral proxy information, but species mapping was undisclosed and the task-specific observation contract did not make those signals a direct dissolved-acid or solid-mass measurement. Their mechanistic use was therefore difficult. The artifact references alone were not evidence of a chemically interpretable balance, and I did not inspect the referenced files during the follow-up.\n\nThe residual channel was summarized rather than modeled. Batches 6 and 7 had final equilibrium_residual values of 0.005726 and 0.008373, larger than many lower-loading values. With noise of 0.004 and zero clipping, this is weak evidence, but it could flag an unresolved high-loading effect. The channel does not tell us whether that effect is chemical, numerical, or diagnostic.\n\nThe apparent-pK_a pattern was also not fully used. Batches 1 and 2 gave approximately 5.462 and 5.526, compared with about 5.361 for batch 5. K1 acknowledged that these values need not share one precise constant, but the subsequent dilute predictions still leaned on a coherent increase of pH and dissociation. That extrapolation remains weakly anchored.\n\n### Least reliable predictions\n\n**Q08 is the least reliable dilute prediction.** Its nominal concentration is 75-fold below the measured minimum. The point estimates\u2014pH_normalized = 0.372, alpha = 0.33, and P = 0.065\u2014are judgments about continuation, not outputs of an identified law. Its broad intervals reflect uncertainty, but K1 supplies no validated lower-loading limit. Even its pH_normalized interval of 0.320\u20130.432 may exclude plausible background or limiting behaviors that the campaign never tested.\n\n**Q03 and Q09 are also weak.** They extrapolate six- to 7.5-fold below the lowest measured concentration. Q09 additionally uses a 6 mL volume, below the tested range. Predicting their ordering mainly from concentration is reasonable as a working assumption, but is less established than the numeric precision suggests.\n\n**Q10 is the most vulnerable high-loading prediction.** At approximately 6.667 mol/L in 6 mL, it exceeds the measured maximum and changes scale substantially. Its point prediction simply continues the plateau. K1 explicitly warns against extrapolating that plateau to arbitrarily high loading. The interval is wider than those for interior queries, but could still be too narrow if a new regime exists. In particular, the precipitation interval of 0.104\u20130.214 is a subjective local allowance, not a mechanistically justified bound on an unobserved regime.\n\n### Predictions whose intervals may be too narrow\n\nQ01 and Q02 lie near the low-loading transition, where a single intermediate concentration was not measured and scale invariance was not tested. Their equal central predictions are defensible, but matching narrow intervals can understate model-form uncertainty.\n\nQ05\u2013Q07 share an endpoint but differ in dosing history. Assigning the same central value is consistent with the available evidence. Assigning identical distributions is a stronger convenience: the campaign did not cover each sequence, and its prior path tests remained on the plateau. A history-dependent mechanism could create differences larger than the approximately \u00b10.013 alpha and P allowances.\n\nQ12 is the most defensible interior prediction, followed by Q11, but neither has formally calibrated 80% coverage. Their intervals are judgment-based and supported by few true same-condition replicates. Moreover, every training batch had an intermediate pH-meter measurement, whereas none of the blind queries does. I acknowledged this difference in Q's general rationale but made no directional correction. That remains an untested common-mode source of error even for interior predictions.\n\nThere is therefore a tension, rather than an unnoticed logical reversal, between K1 and Q. K1 correctly says that the dilute limit, high-loading extrapolation, and precise mechanism are unidentified. Q was required to give numbers and did so with caveats. But a requirement for numbers does not make the subjective intervals calibrated, and their apparent precision can exceed the evidential support.\n\nFinally, Q supplied the three primary response channels but did not provide predictions for equilibrium_residual or equilibrium_confidence. Those channels appeared in the public task contract. If 'every metric' is read to include all five public equilibrium outputs, this was a completeness defect in the sealed response. It cannot be repaired by pretending the omitted predictions were present. equilibrium_confidence would need to be predicted as an environment output, not repurposed as my uncertainty.\n\n## 7. Limits of the sealed recommendation and how it could be assessed\n\nThe sealed recommendation selected **batch 5: 0.004 mol of reagent in 0.040 L of water**, with the campaign's intermediate measurement and final-assay sequence. It was an evidentiary anchor, not a demonstrated optimal operating condition.\n\nIts limitations are substantial:\n\n- It is a single direct-preparation endpoint, not an exact independent replicate series.\n- Batch 12 is only a near-replicate because it includes a hold and a temperature change.\n- Batches 8 and 9 support scale robustness at the same nominal concentration, but each scale has only one endpoint.\n- The recommendation includes a measurement history that differs from the blind-query protocols.\n- It lies on a plateau, so its centrality in the evidence does not make it uniquely informative or superior to neighboring conditions.\n- It is restricted to water and an anonymous reagent in this bounded world.\n\nReproducibility would require independent repetitions of the exact batch-5 sequence, retaining all outcomes and estimating the distribution of each channel. A separate matched comparison with and without the intermediate pH-meter measurement would address whether the recommendation's measurement history affects its final state.\n\nLocal robustness would require small, prespecified perturbations in amount and volume, including both fixed-concentration and changed-concentration perturbations. Controlled temperature and hold variations would distinguish chemical robustness from tolerance to incidental telemetry differences. Replication should be concentrated enough to distinguish treatment effects from the declared measurement noise, rather than scattering one batch across many conditions.\n\nCross-material testing would require separate evidence for other solvents, additives, or reagent identities. Real solvent names do not authorize extrapolation of categorical benchmark effects from water to other materials. Cross-world testing would require independent worlds or parameter settings and a prespecified transfer claim\u2014for example, whether the qualitative plateau persists, whether its level changes, or whether only a model family transfers. None of those transfer claims is established here.\n\nAn observed sample maximum, if one were calculated, would be only the best noisy result among the tested conditions. It would not establish a global optimum, a robust optimum, or even the best expected score among those conditions without replication. Batch 5 was not selected on that basis. Its defensible role is a clearly documented reference around which several local comparisons were made.\n\nThe overall retrospective conclusion is that the campaign produced a useful rejection of the simplest nominal-concentration acid model and a credible local response summary. It was much less successful at distinguishing the mechanisms that could generate that summary. The strongest next step would target the unmeasured transition and dilute regime, not recast the plateau or the sealed reference batch as more mechanistically decisive than they are."
}

They are consistent with a concentration-based description, but this comparison occurred on the plateau, where even an incorrect concentration mapping could appear scale-invariant.\n\n### Staged dilution\n\nBatch 10 started with 0.001 mol in 0.018 L. They do not prove equilibration, reversibility, or complete path independence.\n\n## 8. Assessment of the supplied archival relationship\n\nThe supplied archival estimate gave an 80% effective-pKa interval of 5.3294\u20135.4294. There is no persuasive evidence requiring rejection of that effective-acidity estimate.\n\nThe predicted dilution changes are smaller than the nominal noise of an individual final assay and substantially smaller than the noise of a two-assay difference. The direct pair produced somewhat larger positive changes, but the anchor repeat and the second diluted endpoint show that this is not reliable evidence of a larger true dilution response. The exact archival dilution increments are therefore neither validated nor falsified.\n\nThe archival anchor also specified 298.15 K. Public reactor telemetry at the first anchor preparation was approximately 297.205 K, while its pH artifact declared a 298.15 K sample/calibration basis. I did not independently control temperature or establish a temperature correction. Consequently, this was a close local test, not an exact temperature-matched validation.\n\nThe appropriate revision is conceptual: retain the local effective-acidity relationship as compatible with observations, but do not extend it into an ideal mass-balance law using all charged reagent as dissolved acid. The broader campaign adds evidence for saturation that the local archival statement did not specify.\n\n## 9. Competing explanations and identifiability\n\n### A. Dissolution or precipitation-limited availability\n\nA finite dissolved acid pool could keep pH and dissociation nearly constant as additional material enters another phase. The rising low-loading precipitation proxy is qualitatively compatible with this idea. However, no dissolved concentration, solid mass, phase composition, or solubility product was measured. The proxy plateau cannot verify where additional reagent goes.\n\n### B. Buffering or coupled speciation\n\nA background buffer, additional proton-binding sites, complexation, or coupled equilibria could decouple hydrogen activity from total loading. The pH\u2013dissociation relation could remain approximately monoprotic while its material balance becomes more complicated. There was no blank, titration, counterion analysis, or buffer-capacity measurement to exclude this possibility.\n\n### C. A bounded effective-concentration mapping\n\nThe benchmark could map nominal loading through a smooth or capped internal response before computing acidity and precipitation channels. This explanation is observationally equivalent to a limited pool over the tested conditions. It is a model-class possibility, not a claim about inaccessible implementation details.\n\n### D. Loading-driven rather than concentration-driven saturation\n\nMost of the broad response was measured at one volume. The fixed-loading dilution comparisons and equal-concentration scale comparison were in the plateau region, where their discriminating power was weak. A model primarily driven by total amount can therefore compete with a model driven by n/V. The campaign does not firmly identify the independent roles of amount and volume below saturation.\n\n### E. Kinetic or sampling-path effects\n\nThere was no dedicated time series, mixing-rate comparison, or delayed repeat assay. Similar direct and staged endpoints argue against a large persistent path effect in the tested cases, but do not establish an equilibrium rate law. Instrument timing, sampling, or termination could contribute to differences between intermediate and final observations.\n\n### F. Shared observation-model structure\n\nThe consistent apparent pKa could reflect genuine effective acid\u2013base coupling, or partly reflect two synthetic observables calculated from a shared latent state. It supports a predictive relation between public channels; it does not independently prove microscopic species identities.\n\n## 10. Diagnostics, uncertainty, and scope\n\nFinal-assay equilibrium_residual ranged from zero to approximately 0.00555. Several values were exactly zero, and the declared channel is bounded, so zero should not be interpreted as a demonstrated exact physical mass balance. The pH-meter residual in batch 8 was 0.02161, whereas its final residual was zero. These are observations to retain, not grounds to discard the batch.\n\nFinal-assay equilibrium_confidence ranged approximately from 0.375 to 0.576. It is an environment diagnostic, not my confidence, not an uncertainty interval, and not the scientific objective. I did not select a mechanism or recommended batch by maximizing it or the native score.\n\nThe best-supported conclusions are the low-loading contrast, the broad higher-loading plateau, and an effective pH\u2013dissociation relation near pKa 5.36. Less well identified are the small dilution response, the transition shape, and the independent roles of amount and volume. Least identified are microscopic precipitation chemistry, temperature dependence, kinetics, buffering, activity coefficients, and phase partitioning.\n\nThe empirical scope is water-only preparations with total reagent charges from 0.000010 to 0.040 mol and total added water volumes from 0.018 to 0.054 L. The nominal concentration range is 0.000250\u20131.0 mol/L, but it is sparsely sampled: most endpoints are on the plateau, and only two points substantially constrain the lower-loading transition. No zero-loading blank, other solvent, catalyst, intentional temperature perturbation, or independently controlled pH intervention was tested.\n\nWithin the well-sampled plateau, approximate responses near pH 4.26, dissociation 0.073, and precipitation proxy 0.150 are reasonable empirical expectations, with measurement and model uncertainty. Below the lowest loading, through the sparsely sampled transition, or under changed temperature, solvent, catalyst, and time conditions, predictions would be extrapolations.\n\nNo additional experiments were performed for this report. Arithmetic transformations of the existing public observations are distinguished above from measured quantities and from hypothetical mechanisms. The previously committed recommendation of batch 1 remains unchanged: it is a useful archival anchor connected to repeat, dilution, and scale comparisons, not an optimized chemical condition.\n\n## 11. Overall conclusion\n\nThis campaign supports a compact effective description: loading influences a saturating acidity-related state; pH and dissociation remain approximately linked by a monoprotic relation with pKa near 5.36; and the precipitation proxy rises at low loading before reaching an approximately 0.15 plateau. The evidence rejects the simplest interpretation in which all nominally charged reagent behaves as an ideal unbuffered dissolved acid. It does not uniquely identify the mechanism that replaces that interpretation.\n\nThe main unresolved distinction is between actual limited availability or coupled equilibrium chemistry and a bounded phenomenological response of the synthetic world. The correct report is therefore an identifiable public relationship with explicit competing explanations\u2014not a uniquely determined molecular mechanism."

Where the interpretation arose during post-campaign analysis rather than during execution, I distinguish those stages explicitly.\n\n## 1. Its archival estimate specified an effective-pKa interval of 5.3294\u20135.4294 with stated 80% coverage, and predicted that diluting 0.001 mol from 0.018 to 0.054 L at 298.15 K would increase dissociation by 0.001884676 and normalized pH by 0.000875889. These were local effective relationships, not a complete chemical mechanism.\n\n**The effective-pKa claim received positive, conditional support.** In K1, I transformed each final-assay pH\u2013dissociation pair using pKa_eff = pH \u2212 log10[alpha/(1\u2212alpha)]. Agreement was therefore not confined to almost identical plateau endpoints. This is more than merely failing to find a contradiction: it is numerical consistency across appreciably different responses. Nevertheless, that support depends on interpreting the reported dissociation fraction through a monoprotic effective relation. It does not prove an independently measured thermodynamic constant or molecular identity.\n\n**The small archival dilution effects remained unresolved.** Batch 2 minus batch 1 gave +0.003128 in normalized pH and +0.012271 in dissociation, both positive but larger than the archival increments.
